For adding at p (d=+1 from 0 to1),
delta_add_p[t]:

```
def soft_objective(x, alpha, n):
    c = corr_full(x,n)
    cm = c.max()
    z=alpha*(c-cm)
    # stable logsumexp: max z=0
    lse = np.log(np.exp(z).sum())
    return float(cm + lse/alpha)
```
If alpha small, z could be negative large, exp underflow to 0; if one exp 1 sum >0. Good.
Gradient:
```
def soft_gradient(x, alpha, n):
    c=corr_full(x,n); cm=c.max(); z=alpha*(c-cm); ez=np.exp(z); w=ez/ez.sum()
    G=np.zeros(n)
    G += w[n-1]*(1-2*x)
    for p in range(1,n):
        wp=w[n-1+p]; wn=w[n-1-p]
        if wp==0 and wn==0: continue
        if n-p>0:
            G[:n-p] += wn*(1 - x[p:])
            G[p:] += wp*(1 - 2*x[:n-p]) - wn*x[:n-p]
    return G
```

Let's detail binary all-pairs.

```
def build_add_matrix(h,n):
    L=2*n-1; center=n-1; A=np.zeros((n,L), dtype=np.float64)
    for p in range(1,n):
        # m=+p
        if p < n:
            A[p:, center+p] += 1.0 - h[:n-p]   # first for rows p..n-1, partner row p-? h[i-p] = h[0:n-p]
            A[:n-p, center+p] -= h[p:]          # second for rows 0..n-p-1, partner h[i+p]
        # m=-p
        A[:n-p, center-p] += 1.0 - h[p:]       # first for rows 0..n-p-1, partner h[i+p]
        A[p:, center-p] -= h[:n-p]             # second for rows p..n-1, partner h[i-p]
    return A
```
Check rows alignment: For m=+p first: rows i=p..n-1, partner i-p from 0..n-p-1 => vector h[:n-p]. Good. second rows i=0..n-p-1, partner i+p h[p:]. Good.
For m=-p first: rows i=0..n-p-1, partner i+p h[p:] => +(1-h[p:]). second rows i=p..n-1, partner i-p h[:n-p] => -h[:n-p]. Good.
If fractional? formula for exact delta flipping from 0 to1? Actually if h_i maybe not 0, and flip 0->1 only if h_i=0. Formula uses h_p? no. It's exact for binary neighbor values. For binary neighbor values 0/1, okay. For fractional, not.

```
def binary_best_swap(h,n,deadline):
    hb = make_binary(h,n) maybe if already binary
    c = corr_counts(hb)
    cur = c.max()
    best = hb.copy(); bestc=cur
    center=n-1; L=2*n-1
    ones = np.where(hb>0.5)[0]; zeros = np.where(hb<0.5)[0]
    accepted=0
    for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       if len(ones)==0 or len(zeros)==0: break
       A = build_add_matrix(hb,n)
       C = len(zeros); M = len(ones)
       # Chunk size adaptive to memory: target ~2_000_000 floats
       chunk = max(1, min(C, 2000000//(M*L))) maybe.
       local_best = cur; bj=-1; bi=-1
       for start in range(0, C, chunk):
           I = zeros[start:start+chunk]
           cc = c[None,:] + A[I]  # c x L
           sub = cc[None,:,:] - A[ones][None,:,:]  # c x M x L
           maxM = sub.max(axis=2)  # c x M
           Cm = sub.shape[0]; Mm=sub.shape[1]
           flat = sub.reshape(Cm*Mm, L)
           # pair indices
           pair_ar = np.arange(Cm*Mm)
           t1 = (center + I[:,None] - ones[None,:]).reshape(-1)
           t2 = center - t1  # since center + ones - I = center - (I-ones)
           v1 = flat[pair_ar, t1]
           v2 = flat[pair_ar, t2]
           newmax = np.maximum(maxM.reshape(-1), np.maximum(v1+1.0, v2+1.0))
           # if newmax < cur? We want min; maybe local_best
           minv, idx = newmax.min(), int(np.argmin(newmax))
           if minv < local_best - 1e-12:
               local_best=minv; i_idx=idx//M; j_idx=idx%M; bi=I[i_idx]; bj=ones[j_idx]
       if bj>=0:
           # apply swap
           hb[bi]=1.0; hb[bj]=0.0
           # update sets: moving zero bi to ones, one bj to zeros
           # Could rebuild sets each sweep to avoid costly list updates? nbig n small.
           ones = np.where(hb>0.5)[0]; zeros = np.where(hb<0.5)[0]
           c = corr_counts(hb)
           cur = c.max()
           if cur < bestc:
               bestc=cur; best=hb.copy()
       else:
           # no improving swap; maybe try equal moves? To escape, consider a few random swaps that don't worsen too much? Or restart.
           break
    return best, bestc*2/n? We can return counts and convert.
```

```
def lag_gradient(h, m, n):
    G=np.zeros(n); L=... center=n-1
    # m can positive, negative, zero
    if m==0: return 1-2h
    p=abs(m)
    if m>0:
       # c_p: G[:n-p] += -h[p:]; G[p:] += 1-h[:n-p]
    else:
       # c_-p: G[:n-p] += 1-h[p:]; G[p:] += -h[:n-p]
    return G
```

```
def resample_h(h,n, nh):
    if nh==n: return h.copy()
    xo=np.linspace(0,2,n)
    xn=np.linspace(0,2,nh)
    hh=np.interp(xn,xo,h)
    # interp may exceed [0,1]? if h in yes.
    return project_feasible(hh, nh/2)
```

```
def active_descent(h,n,deadline,rng=None,max_iter=10000):
  best=project; bestc=exact; stall=0
  settings=[(0.1,20,0.05),(0.5,50,0.03),(1.0,100,0.02),(5.0,300,0.01)]
  for tol,ka,eta0 in settings:
    iter...
    while time<deadline and stall<50:
      c=M...
      sel = np.array(np.unique(np.concatenate((np.argsort(c)[::-1][:ka], np.where(c>=M-tol)[0])))) # if not too many
      G=sum lag_gradient...
      if norm small break
      d=-G/norm
      improved=False
      for eta in [eta0,eta0/2,... eta0/512]:
         hnew=project(h+eta*d)
         cnew=exact
         if cnew < bestc - 1e-14:
            h=hnew; bestc=cnew; stall=0; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         stall += 1
         if rng: small random project? maybe
      if stall>=20: break
  return best,bestc
```

```
def project_feasible(x, target):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    n=x.size; lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
```

```
def project_feasible(x,target):
   x=np.asarray(x,float)
   if target<=0: zeros; if target>=x.size: ones
   lo,hi=-2.0,2.0
   for _ in range(80):
      mid=(lo+hi)/2
      s=np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0))
      if s<target: lo=mid
      else: hi=mid
   return np.clip(x+(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
```

Let's draft code structure in mind:
```
import numpy as np, time
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog  # linprog maybe optional
_TARGET=0.0
def _set_target(t): global _TARGET; _TARGET=t
def _sum_constraint(x): return x.sum() - _TARGET

def corr_counts(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
def exact_c5(h,n):
    return float(np.max(corr_counts(h)) * (2.0/n))
def project_feasible(x,target): ...
def make_binary(h,n): ...
def resample(h, n, nh): ...
def soft_objective(x, alpha, n): ...
def grad_weights(h, c, w, n): ...
def soft_gradient(x, alpha, n): c=corr; cm=c.max(); z...; w=...; return grad_weights
def slsqp_soft(h, n, alpha, maxiter):
   _set_target(n/2.0)
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_constraint}]
   try:
      res=minimize(soft_objective, h, jac=soft_gradient, args=(alpha,n), method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-14,'disp':False})
      return project_feasible(res.x, n/2.0)
   except Exception:
      return h
```

```
def active_weights(c, tol, max_active):
    L=len(c); center=L//2; M=c.max()
    order=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    sel = order[:max_active]
    # also include all within tol (except maybe c undefined)

    if near.size > (L//2): # avoid huge
        sel = near[:max_active] maybe? But we want top. Actually use union but cap total top maybe 2*max_active.
    else:
        sel = np.union1d(sel, near)

    w=np.zeros(L); w[sel]=1.0/sel.size; return w
```

Then active_descent:
```
def active_descent(h,n,deadline,max_iter=5000,seed=0):
    best=project; bestc=exact; rng=RandomState(seed)
    settings=[(0.25,20,0.08),(1.0,50,0.05),(4.0,150,0.03),(20.0,400,0.015)]
    for tol,ka,eta0 in settings:
       stall=0
       while time.time()<deadline and stall<30:
           c=corr_counts(best); M=c.max()
           w=active_weights(c,tol,ka)
           G=grad_weights(best,c,w,n)
           gnorm=np.linalg.norm(G)
           if gnorm<1e-12: stall+=5; break
           d=-G/gnorm
           improved=False
           for f in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.015625,0.0078125]:
                eta=eta0*f
                hnew=project_feasible(best+eta*d, n/2)
                cnew=exact_c5(hnew,n)
                if cnew < bestc - 1e-14:
                    best=hnew; bestc=cnew; stall=0; improved=True; break
           if not improved:
              stall += 1
              # optional tiny random perturbation projected
              if stall % 5 ==0:
                 best=project_feasible(best + 0.01*rng.randn(n), n/2)
      # maybe after setting if no improve break? continue to next
    return best,bestc
```
Potential issue: The direction d normalized may be scaled too large/small. If eta0 0.08, normalized gradient components maybe up to 1; step 0.08. If exact not improve at 0.08, smaller may. Good. def continuous_polish(h, n, deadline, rng, label='main', full=True):
```
    h=project(h,n/2)
    best=h.copy(); bestc=exact
    alphas = [20,100,500,2000,10000] if full else [20,200,2000]
    maxiter_main = 600 if n<=260 else 250
    for a in alphas:
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       starts=0
       # start current best
       for mode in [0,1,2]: # current, small perturb, binary
          if time.time() > deadline: break
          if mode==0: start=best.copy()
          elif mode==1: start=project(best + 0.03*rng.randn(n),n/2)
          else: start=make_binary(best,n)
          # maybe if start c not worse than bestc + 0.001? For binary always worse maybe; but can lead.
          maxit = maxiter_main
          if time.time() > deadline: break
          # For high alpha and n large reduce
          if a>=10000 and n>300: maxit=min(maxit,100)
          h2=slsqp_soft(start,n,a,maxit)
          # maybe snap small values if improves?
          c2=exact
          if c2 < bestc - 1e-14:
             best=h2; bestc=c2
       # after each alpha, active descent short? Maybe after all.
    # final active descent until deadline
    best,bestc=active_descent(best,n,deadline,max_iter=2000,seed=int(label hash?) can't hash string; use rng int)
    return best,bestc
```

Implementation:
For chunk I:
  base = c + A[I] (C,L)
  score = base - minVal
  top_lags = argpartition(...) shape C x R (lags)
  # Need one index for each top lag: minIdx[top_lags] shape CxR -> j indices in ones array; J = ones[one_pos]
  # candidate count C*R. We might have duplicates; can ignore.
  # Compute deltas for candidates:
  ACand = A[I][:, None, :] shape C,1,L
  AJ = A[J] shape C,R,L
  delta = base[:, None, :] - AJ  # c + A_i - A_j
  # cross t1/t2 for each candidate:
  ii = I[:,None]; jj = J  # CxR
  t1 = center + ii - jj
  t2 = center + jj - ii
  flat = delta.reshape(-1,L)
  idx = np.arange(flat.shape[0])
  v1 = flat[idx, t1.ravel()]; v2=flat[idx,t2.ravel()]
  maxM = delta.max(axis=2).ravel()

Algorithm:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,max_sweeps=1000,restarts=5,seed=42):
  hb=make_binary; best=; bestc=; cur=
  for restart in range(restarts):
    if time>deadline: break
    if restart>0:
       # perturb by k random swaps from best or current?
       cur = make_binary(best)
       for _ in range(rng.randint(2,5)): swap random one/zero; recompute; if new C > bestc + 0.01 maybe revert? Keep if not too worse.
       c=corr(cur); curmax=...
    improved=False
    while time<deadline and sweeps<max_sweeps:
       move = full_best_move(cur,c,ones,zeros)
       if no move: break
       apply; recompute; if newcur < bestc update best
    # if no move and restarts remain, perturb
```

```
def binary_find_move(h,c,ones,zeros,center):
    n=len(h); L=len(c)
    if len(zeros)==0 or len(ones)==0: return None
    A=build_add_matrix(h,n)
    Aones=A[ones]
    C=len(zeros)
    # choose chunk size: max sub floats 2_000_000? For safe memory
    max_elems=2_000_000
    chunk=max(1, min(C, max_elems // (len(ones)*L)))
    bestv=np.inf; besti=-1; bestj=-1
    for start in range(0,C,chunk):
       I=zeros[start:start+chunk]
       base=c[None,:]+A[I]
       sub=base[None,:,:]-Aones[None,:,:]
       Cm=sub.shape[:2]
       maxM=sub.max(axis=2).ravel()
       flat=sub.reshape(Cm[0]*Cm[1], L)
       t1=(center + I[:,None] - ones[None,:]).ravel()
       t2=(center + I[:,None] -? wait t2=center + ones - I)
       idxnp=np.arange(flat.shape[0])
       v1=flat[idxnp,t1]; v2=flat[idxnp,t2]
       newmax=np.maximum(maxM, np.maximum(v1+1,v2+1))
       m=int(np.argmin(newmax)); val=float(newmax[m])
       if val<bestv:
           cm_idx=m // Cm[1]; bestv=val; besti=I[cm_idx]; bestj=ones[m % Cm[1]]
    if bestv < np.inf:
       return int(besti), int(bestj), bestv
    return None
```

```
def binary_perturb(hb, rng, steps):
    h=hb.copy(); ones=...; zeros=...

       if not ones or zeros: break
       a=rng.choice(ones); b=rng.choice(zeros); h[a]=0; h[b]=1; update sets maybe simple rebuild after loop.
```

```
def grad_weights(h, c, w, n):
    G = np.zeros(n)
    center = n-1
    w0 = w[center]
    if w0 != 0.0:
        G += w0 * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
    for p in range(1,n):
        wp = w[center+p]
        wn = w[center-p]
        if wp == 0.0 and wn == 0.0: continue
        a = n - p
        if wn != 0.0:
            G[:a] += wn * (1.0 - h[p:])
        if wp != 0.0:
            G[p:a+p?] Wait earlier full formula combined:
            G[:a] += wn*(1-h[p:]) - wp*h[p:]
            G[p:] += wp*(1-h[:a]) - wn*h[:a]
```
Use combined to reduce ops:
```
        # left slice G[:a] (l=0..a-1)
        G[:a] += wn * (1.0 - h[p:]) - wp * h[p:]
        # right slice G[p:] (l=p..n-1)
        G[p:] += wp * (1.0 - h[:a]) - wn * h[:a]
```

Let's implement `iter_budget`:
```
def iter_budget(n, deadline, cap):
    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem < 10: return 0
    per = 0.06 if n <= 260 else (0.12 if n <= 350 else 0.2)
    it = int(rem / (per * 4.0))
    if it < 20: return 0
    return min(cap, it)
```
For n=250, rem=380, per*4=0.24 ->1583 cap800 ->800. If actual 0.1s/iter, 80s; multiple alphas could total >380? But iter_budget called each start with remaining. Fast binary move function (top-level) `binary_find_fast_move(h, c, zeros, ones, center, R)`:
```
def binary_find_fast_move(h,c,zeros,ones,center,R):
    if zeros.size==0 or ones.size==0: return None
    n=h.size; L=len(c)
    A=build_add_matrix(h,n)
    Aones=A[ones]
    minVal=Aones.min(axis=0)
    minPos=np.argmin(Aones, axis=0)
    base=c[None,:]+A[zeros]
    score=base - minVal[None,:]
    R=min(R,L)
    if R<L:
        top=np.argpartition(score, L-R, axis=1)[:, L-R:]
    else:
        top=np.argsort(score, axis=1)[:, ::-1][:, :R]
    Jpos=minPos[top]  # Z x R
    J=ones[Jpos]
    AJ=A[J]  # Z x R x L
    delta=base[:,None,:] - AJ
    maxM=delta.max(axis=2)
    t1=(center + zeros[:,None] - J).ravel()
    t2=(center + zeros[:,None] +? actually center + J - zeros).ravel()
    flat=delta.reshape(-1,L)
    idxp=np.arange(flat.shape[0])
    v1=flat[idxp,t1]
    v2=flat[idxp,t2]
    newmax=np.maximum(maxM.ravel(), np.maximum(v1+1.0, v2+1.0))
    m=int(np.argmin(newmax))
    if m >=0:
       r=m//R; col=m%R
       return int(zeros[r]), int(J[r,col]), float(newmax[m])
    return None
```
Need if R may be 0? no. `argpartition` kth = L-R. If R=0? no.
Memory for `AJ=A[J]` with J 2D returns a copy. For n=350, 175*48*699=5.87M float 47MB; plus delta same? `base[:,None,:]` broadcast maybe not copy until subtract creates delta 47MB. `AJ` 47, total 94, okay maybe. For n=400, 200*48*799=7.67M 61MB each, total 122, maybe okay but close. We can skip binary for n>350 or reduce R. In binary_local_search, set R based on n: if n<=250 R=64, elif <=320 R=48, else R=24/32. For n=350 R=32 ->175*32*699=3.9M 31MB each total62. Good. Use R maybe 48 for <=300, 32 for <=350, 16 otherwise.

This fast move only evaluates lags where at that lag a minimizing one gives max. It may miss as discussed. But with high R (64 for n=250, out of 499 lags) and random restarts, likely okay. Could also if no fast move and time remains, perform a few random restarts. If a true improving move exists with no-cross lag not in top 64, it means at least 64 lags with no-cross score much higher; those candidate moves likely not improve? Maybe possible, but rare. To be safer, we can when fast stuck, enlarge R dynamically or run full one verification? We can include a simple full verification only if no fast move and time > 10s: run `binary_find_full_move` maybe not too many lines? Could be lengthy. Alternatively do a random perturbation and continue. Might not beat if local optimum. But continuous polish likely main.

Maybe implement a full verification using the same candidate code but with J=ones (R = len(ones))? The fast code can be generalized: if `R >= len(ones)`? Actually full all pairs uses for each zero all lags and all ones; candidate code uses top lags per zero but not all ones. Now, `binary_local_search` fast:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,max_sweeps=2000,restarts=4,seed=42):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    hb=make_binary(h,n); c=corr_counts(hb); cur=c.max(); best=hb; bestc=cur; dx=2.0/n
    R = 64 if n<=250 else (48 if n<=320 else 24)
    for restart in range(restarts):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       if restart>0:
           cur=make_binary(best,n)
           for _ in range(rng.randint(1,4)):
               ones=np.where(cur>0.5)[0]; zeros=np.where(cur<0.5)[0]
               if ones.size==0 or zeros.size==0: break
               a=rng.choice(ones); b=rng.choice(zeros); cur[a]=0; cur[b]=1
           c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
           if cur*dx > bestc + 0.02:
              cur=make_binary(best,n); c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
       sweeps=0
       while time.time()<deadline and sweeps<max_sweeps:
           sweeps+=1
           zeros=np.where(cur<0.5)[0]; ones=np.where(cur>0.5)[0]
           fp=binary_find_fast_move(cur,c,zeros,ones,n-1,R)
           if fp is None: break
           i,j,nv=fp
           if nv >= cur - 1e-12: break
           cur[i]=1.0; cur[j]=0.0
           c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
           if cur*dx < bestc:
              bestc=cur*dx; best=cur.copy()
    return best,bestc
```
Rebuilding zeros/ones each sweep O(n), fine. `binary_find_fast_move` builds A each sweep O(n^2). For n=250, fast move memory 125*64*499=4M, constructing A 2MB. Per sweep maybe 0.05s; 1000 sweeps 50s. Good.
Potential bug: In `binary_find_fast_move`, `A[J]` with J shape (Z,R) returns shape (Z,R,L). If `J` contains repeated columns, okay. `base[:,None,:] - AJ` shape. `t1=(center + zeros[:,None] - J).ravel()` center + i - j (lag index). `t2=(center + J - zeros[:,None]).ravel()`. Good. If J is int. `np.argmin(Aones,axis=0)` returns int indices 0..M-1. `Jpos = minPos[top]` where top shape ZxR with values lags 0..L-1. Good.
If R==L, `top=np.argsort(score, axis=1)[:, ::-1][:, :R]`. If L large and R maybe smaller, argpartition. Need ensure `L-R` nonnegative. If R=0? no.
`argpartition` with kth=L-R when R maybe 1: kth=L-1, top = score partition at L-1, slice [-1:]? Actually slice `[:, L-R:]` = `[:, L-1:]` one column, the max? The partition puts element at kth sorted; elements after are larger? For kth=L-1, the element at index L-1 is largest, after none? Actually slice [L-1:] includes index L-1. Good. If R=2, kth=L-2, indices L-2,L-1 are two largest (unsorted). Good.

```
def active_descent(h,n,deadline,seed=0,max_iter=2000):
    t0? use time
    target=n*0.5
    cur=project_feasible(h,target)
    best=cur.copy(); bc=c5_from_counts(corr_counts(best),n)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    settings = [(0.25,32,0.08),(1.0,64,0.05),(4.0,160,0.03),(16.0,400,0.015)]
    for tol,ka,eta0 in settings:
        stall=0
        while time.time()<deadline and stall<40:
            c=corr_counts(cur); M=c.max()
            order=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
            near=np.where(c >= M - max(tol, 1e-9))[0]
            if near.size > max(ka,1):
                # choose top min(max(ka, near.size?), but ai: keep ka top overall? We'll use top ka if near larger
                sel=order[:min(ka, near.size)]
            else:
                sel=near
            # ensure at least top 1
            if sel.size ==0: sel=order[:1]
            w=np.zeros(c.size); w[sel]=1.0/sel.size
            G=grad_weights(cur,c,w,n)
            gn=np.linalg.norm(G)
            if gn < 1e-14: stall+=10; continue
            d=-G/gn
            improved=False
            for f in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.0156250,0.0078125):
                nm=project_feasible(cur + eta0*f*d, target)
                cm=corr_counts(nm); cv=cm.max()*(2.0/n)
                if cv < bc - 1e-14:
                    cur=nm; bc=cv; best=nm.copy(); stall=0; improved=True; break
            if not improved:
                stall += 1
                if stall in (5,10,15,20,25):
                    cand=project_feasible(cur + 0.02*rng.randn(n), target)
                    cv=c5...; if cv <= bc + 0.001: cur=cand
            if time.time()>deadline: break
    return best,bestc? we named bc, return bc.
```

```
def continuous_polish(h, n, deadline, rng, seed, full=True):

       hs=... maybe no
```

Now, resample:
```
def resample(h,n,nh):
    if nh==n: return project_feasible(h,n*0.5)
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,nh)
    hh=np.interp(xn,xo,h)
    return project_feasible(hh,nh*0.5)
```
If n small and nh large, okay.

Fallback:
```
def initial_fallback(n,seed):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    h=np.zeros(n); idx=rng.permutation(n); h[idx[:n//2]]=1.0; return h
```
If n odd, n//2 not n/2; but fallback only if no parent and likely we set n even 250. Use n even.

Implement:
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    if target <=0: return np.zeros(n)
    if target >=n: return np.ones(n)
    # quick path: if already close
    y=np.clip(x,0,1); s=y.sum()
    if abs(s-target) < 1e-12: return y
    span=max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(x))))
    lo,hi=-span-1.0, span+1.0
    for _ in range(45):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       val=np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0))
       if val < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    return np.clip(x+(lo+hi)*0.5,0.0,1.0)
```
This 45*projection. If x already feasible, quick path returns. In active line search, x=cur+eta*d with cur feasible; after clip sum might be near target; quick path may not trigger if sum off due to d. It will clip and sum. Bisection still. Could optimize by using y and adjust:
```
y=np.clip(x,0,1); s=y.sum(); diff=target-s
if abs(diff)<1e-12 return y
if diff>0:
   room=1-y; sroom=room.sum(); if sroom<abs(diff): ...; y += diff/sroom*room; return clip
else: y += diff/s_y*y ...
```
This one-step water fill may not be exact if after adding all room hit bound; need iterate but few. We can combine: do a few proportional adjustments, then bisection if not exact. This is faster.
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
  x=np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size
  if target<=0... 
  y=np.clip(x,0,1); s=y.sum(); diff=target-s
  if abs(diff)<1e-12: return y
  for _ in range(20):
     if abs(diff)<1e-12: break
     if diff>0:
        sroom=(1-y).sum()
        if sroom < 1e-14: break
        y += diff/sroom*(1-y)
     else:
        sy=y.sum()
        if sy<1e-14: break
        y += diff/sy*y
     y=np.clip(y,0,1)
     diff=target-y.sum()
  if abs(diff)>1e-12:
     # bisection as fallback from y
  return np.clip(y,0,1)
```
This water fill converges in few iterations and avoids 45 per. Need ensure not oscillate.
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    if target <= 0: return np.zeros(n)
    if target >= n: return np.ones(n)
    y=np.clip(x,0,1)
    target=float(target)
    for _ in range(60):
        s=y.sum(); diff=target-s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
        if diff > 0:
            room=1.0-y; sr=room.sum()
            if sr < 1e-15: break
            y = y + diff/sr*room
        else:
            sy=y.sum()
            if sy < 1e-15: break
            y = y + diff/sy*y
        y=np.clip(y,0,1)
    if abs(y.sum()-target) > 1e-10:
        lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
        # ensure f(lo)<target<f(hi); if not widen
        for _ in range(60): ... bisection on original x? Use y? Use mid.
    return np.clip(y,0,1)
```

```
def iter_budget(n, deadline, cap):
    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem < 10: return 0
    base = 0.04 if n <= 220 else (0.08 if n <= 320 else 0.15)
    it = int(rem / (base * 6.0))
    return max(0, min(cap, it))
```

`project_feasible`:
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    if target <= 0.0: return np.zeros(n)
    if target >= float(n): return np.ones(n)
    y=np.clip(x,0.0,1.0)
    target=float(target)
    for _ in range(40):
        s=float(y.sum())
        diff=target-s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
        if diff > 0.0:
            room=1.0-y
            sr=float(room.sum())
            if sr < 1e-15: break
            y=y+(diff/sr)*room
        else:
            sy=float(y.sum())
            if sy < 1e-15: break
            y=y+(diff/sy)*y
        y=np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
    if abs(float(y.sum())-target) > 1e-10:
        lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
        for _ in range(60):
            mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
            val=float(np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0)))
            if val < target: lo=mid
            else: hi=mid
        y=np.clip(x+0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    return y
```
If x far outside >1, bisection lo=-1,hi=1 may not span enough; but quick path from y? Fallback use lo=-max(10, max(abs(x))-1), etc. Could improve:
`span=max(1.0,float(np.max(np.abs(x)))) if n else 1.0; lo=-span-1; hi=span+1`. Use.
Also loop 40 water fill maybe diff not exact if y.sum changes due to clip; ok.

```
def make_binary(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    target=n//2 if n%2==0 else int(n*0.5+0.5)? For even only. Use target=n//2.
    idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1][:target]
    hb=np.zeros(n)
    hb[idx]=1.0
    return hb
```
For odd not used. If n even, target n/2.

`resample`:
```
def resample(h,n,nh):
    if nh==n:
       return project_feasible(h,n*0.5)
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,nh)
    return project_feasible(np.interp(xn,xo,h), nh*0.5)
```

`soft_objective`, `grad_weights`, `soft_gradient`.
In `grad_weights`, use local variables for speed:
```
def grad_weights(h,c,w,n):
    G=np.zeros(n)
    center=n-1
    w0=w[center]
    if w0:
       G += w0*(1.0-2.0*h)
    for p in range(1,n):
       wp=w[center+p]; wn=w[center-p]
       if wp == 0.0 and wn == 0.0: continue
       a=n-p
       hp_r=h[p:]; hm_l=h[:a]
       G[:a] += wn*(1.0-hp_r) - wp*hp_r
       G[p:] += wp*(1.0-hm_l) - wn*hm_l
    return G
```
If wp/wn are small floats, `==0.0` okay. For active many zeros exact. For soft, all weights may be nonzero (underflow some exactly zero). Good.
Potential sign for G[:a] if wp/wn floats: yes. If wn=0, `wn*(1-hp_r)` zero; if wp=0, `-wp*hp_r` zero. Could still compute with zeros? Maybe to save, branch:
```
if wn: G[:a] += wn*(1-hp_r)
if wp: G[:a] -= wp*hp_r
if wn: G[p:] -= wn*hm_l
if wp: G[p:] += wp*(1-hm_l)
```
More lines but avoids ops. Current combined fine, but if both zero skip.

`soft_gradient`:
```
def soft_gradient(x,alpha,n):
    c=corr_counts(x)
    cm=float(c.max())
    z=alpha*(c-cm)
    ez=np.exp(z)
    s=float(ez.sum())
    w=ez/s
    return grad_weights(x,c,w,n)
```
If s=0 (shouldn't) set w center=1.
```
if s <=0: w=np.zeros(L); w[n-1]=1
```
Add.

`soft_objective` similar with s.

`slsqp_soft`:
```
def slsqp_soft(h,n,alpha,maxiter):
    target=n*0.5
    _set_target(target)
    h0=project_feasible(h,target)
    if maxiter <=0: return h0
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(n)]
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_fun,'jac':_sum_jac}]
    try:
       res=minimize(soft_objective,h0,args=(float(alpha),int(n)),method='SLSQP',jac=soft_gradient,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-13,'disp':False})
       hx=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    except Exception:
       hx=h0
    return project_feasible(hx,target)
```
List comprehension `[(0.0,1.0)]*n` shorter. Use that.

`iter_budget`:
```
def iter_budget(n,deadline,cap):
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem < 10.0: return 0
    base=0.04 if n<=220 else (0.08 if n<=320 else 0.15)
    it=int(rem/(base*6.0))
    if it <=0: return 0
    if it > cap: it=cap
    return it
```

`active_descent` code. Use `c5c(c,n)`? c5c takes c,n. In line search, `cv = c5c(cm,n)`. Good.
```
def active_descent(h,n,deadline,seed=0,max_iter=10000):
    target=n*0.5; dx=2.0/n
    best=project_feasible(h,target)
    bestc=c5c(corr_counts(best),n)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    settings=[(0.25,32,0.08),(1.0,64,0.05),(4.0,160,0.03),(16.0,400,0.015)]
    for tol,ka,eta0 in settings:
        cur=best.copy()
        stall=0
        ii=0
        while time.time() < deadline and stall < 45 and ii < max_iter:
            ii += 1
            c=corr_counts(cur); M=float(c.max())
            order=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
            near=np.where(c >= M - max(float(tol),1e-12))[0]
            if near.size > ka:
                sel=order[:ka]
            else:
                sel=near
            if sel.size ==0:
                sel=order[:1]
            w=np.zeros(c.size)
            w[sel]=1.0/float(sel.size)
            G=grad_weights(cur,c,w,n)
            gn=float(np.linalg.norm(G))
            if gn < 1e-14:
                stall += 5
                continue
            d=-G/gn
            improved=False
            for f in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.015625,0.0078125):
                nh_=project_feasible(cur+eta0*f*d,target)
                cv=c5c(corr_counts(nh_),n)
                if cv < bestc - 1e-14:
                    cur=nh_; bestc=cv; best=nh_.copy(); stall=0; improved=True; break
            if not improved:
                stall += 1
                if stall in (6,12,18,24,30) and time.time() < deadline - 5:
                    cand=project_feasible(cur+0.02*rng.randn(n),target)
                    cv=c5c(corr_counts(cand),n)
                    if cv <= bestc + 0.002:
                        cur=cand
    return best,bestc
```
If `near.size > ka`, `order[:ka]` all may be within near? If near.size>ka yes top ka are near because near condition includes first near.size values? Not exactly if there are gaps? `near` includes all c >= M-tol; if size>ka, the top ka values must be >= M-tol because the (ka+1)-th highest is >= M-tol? If near size > ka, the ka-th highest is among near. So order[:ka] near. Good. If near.size<=ka, sel=near could be less than ka; okay. If near empty? M within tol so at least max lag. Good.
`stall in (6,12,...)` tuple; ok.

`build_add_matrix`:
```
def build_add_matrix(h,n):
    L=2*n-1; center=n-1
    A=np.zeros((n,L))
    for p in range(1,n):
        a=n-p
        A[p:,center+p] += 1.0 - h[:a]
        A[:a,center+p] -= h[p:]
        A[:a,center-p] += 1.0 - h[p:]
        A[p:,center-p] -= h[:a]
    return A
```

`binary_find_fast_move`:
```
def binary_find_fast_move(cur,c,zeros,ones,center,R):
    if zeros.size==0 or ones.size==0: return None
    n=cur.size; L=len(c)
    if R > L: R=L
    if R <=0: return None
    A=build_add_matrix(cur,n)
    Aones=A[ones]
    minVal=Aones.min(axis=0)
    minPos=np.argmin(Aones,axis=0)
    base=c[None,:]+A[zeros]
    score=base - minVal[None,:]
    if R < L:
        top=np.argpartition(score, L-R, axis=1)[:, L-R:]
    else:
        top=np.argsort(score, axis=1)[:, ::-1][:, :R]
    Jpos=minPos[top]
    J=ones[Jpos]
    AJ=A[J]
    delta=base[:,None,:] - AJ
    maxM=delta.max(axis=2)
    t1=(center + zeros[:,None] - J).ravel()
    t2=(center + zeros[:,None] + J - zeros[:,None]?)
```
Need t2 formula: center + J - zeros[:,None]. Since J and zeros both shape ZxR, `center + J - zeros[:,None]` ok. Use `.ravel()`.
`flat=delta.reshape(-1,L)`; `idxp=np.arange(flat.shape[0])`; `v1=flat[idxp,t1]; v2=flat[idxp,t2]`; `newmax=np.maximum(maxM.ravel(), np.maximum(v1+1.0, v2+1.0))`; m argmin; if m ... `zr=m//R; rc=m%R; return int(zeros[zr]), int(J[zr,rc]), float(newmax[m])`. If R may be int; yes.
Potential memory: `base[:,None,:] - AJ` creates delta. `base` shape ZxL, AJ ZxR x L. Good.

If `zeros` length 1 and R maybe L, `top` sort. Good.

`binary_local_search`:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,max_sweeps=2000,restarts=4,seed=42):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    hb=make_binary(h,n)
    c=corr_counts(hb); cur=c.max(); bestc=float(cur)*(2.0/n); best=hb.copy()
    if n <= 250: R=64
    elif n <= 320: R=48
    else: R=24
    for restart in range(restarts):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        if restart > 0:
            cur=best.copy()
            for _ in range(int(rng.randint(1,4))):
                ones=np.where(cur > 0.5)[0]; zeros=np.where(cur < 0.5)[0]
                if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0: break
                a=int(rng.choice(ones)); b=int(rng.choice(zeros))
                cur[a]=0.0; cur[b]=1.0
            c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
            if float(cur)*(2.0/n) > bestc + 0.05:
                cur=best.copy(); c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
        sweeps=0
        while time.time() < deadline and sweeps < max_sweeps:
            sweeps += 1
            zeros=np.where(cur < 0.5)[0]; ones=np.where(cur > 0.5)[0]
            mv=binary_find_fast_move(cur,c,zeros,ones,n-1,R)
            if mv is None: break
            i,j,nv=mv
            if nv >= cur - 1e-12: break
            cur[i]=1.0; cur[j]=0.0
            c=corr_counts(cur); cur=c.max()
            cv=float(cur)*(2.0/n)
            if cv < bestc:
                bestc=cv; best=cur.copy()
    return best,bestc
```
If `nv` exact maybe slightly less than cur but due to float; okay. If `nv` maybe >cur? We take min so if min < cur accept. If min >= cur break.

Note: In `binary_find_fast_move`, we only evaluate R top no-cross lags for each zero; the global min candidate `newmax` might not be less than cur even if there is an improving move among lower lags. Then binary breaks. Could random restarts help. `restarts=4`, but if initial local for fast, maybe no improvement. We can increase restarts/time. For continuous, ok.

```
if R*2 <= 200:

else break
```

Now, `continuous_polish` code:
```
def continuous_polish(h,n,deadline,rng,seed,full=True):
    target=n*0.5
    best=project_feasible(h,target)
    bestc=c5c(corr_counts(best),n)
    if full:
        alphas=[20.0,100.0,500.0,2000.0,8000.0]
        cap=250 if n<=300 else 100
    else:
        alphas=[20.0,200.0,2000.0]
        cap=120 if n<=300 else 60
    improved_stall=0
    for idx,a in enumerate(alphas):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        it=iter_budget(n,deadline,cap)
        if it <= 0: break
        start=best.copy()
        if idx == 1 or (idx == 2 and time.time() < deadline - 80):
             # perturb start before one extra? maybe do current then perturb? To keep code short, only current.
        h2=slsqp_soft(start,n,a,it)
        cv=c5c(corr_counts(h2),n)
        if cv < bestc - 1e-14:
            best=h2; bestc=cv; improved_stall=0
        else:
            improved_stall += 1
            if improved_stall >= 2 and time.time() < deadline - 80:
                start2=project_feasible(best + 0.03*rng.randn(n), target)
                it2=iter_budget(n,deadline,cap//2)
                if it2 > 0:
                    h2=slsqp_soft(start2,n,a,it2)
                    cv=c5c(corr_counts(h2),n)
                    if cv < bestc - 1e-14:
                        best=h2; bestc=cv; improved_stall=0
    if time.time() < deadline - 5:
        best,bestc=active_descent(best,n,deadline,seed=seed+11,max_iter=5000)
    return best,bestc
```
The perturbation after 2 stalls may waste; but okay. `improved_stall` counts consecutive alphas without improvement; perturb only once. Could need multiple. Fine.
`slsqp_soft` projects start; best already projected.
`active_descent` can take entire remaining, maybe SLSQP not use full. Good.

Potential issue: In `continuous_polish`, if SLSQP from best improves, `h2` maybe not exact bestc? We update. If SLSQP fails and returns h0, cv same. If active descent then improves.

Maybe after SLSQP with high alpha, h2 has many fractions; active descent uses exact. Good.

```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); budget=float(budget_s); hard=t0+budget-5.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
       h0=np.array(initial_h_values,dtype=float).copy()
       n0=int(h0.size)
    except Exception:
       n0=250; h0=np.zeros(n0); idx=rng.permutation(n0); h0[idx[:n0//2]]=1.0
    if n0 % 2 != 0 or n0 < 50 or n0 > 600:
       h0=resample(h0,n0,250); n0=250
    best_h=project_feasible(h0,n0*0.5)
    best_c=c5c(corr_counts(best_h),n0)
    # primary
    dl1=t0+0.34*budget
    if dl1 > hard - 60: dl1=hard-60
    best_h,best_c=continuous_polish(best_h,n0,dl1,rng,seed,full=True)
    # binary at n0 if even
    if n0 % 2 == 0:
       dl2=t0+0.50*budget
       if dl2 > hard - 120: dl2=hard-120
       if time.time() < dl2 - 5:
          hb,hc=binary_local_search(best_h,n0,dl2,3000,4,seed+1)
          if hc < best_c:
             best_h,best_c=hb,hc
          elif hc <= best_c + 0.015:
             dl3=t0+0.58*budget
             if dl3 > hard - 90: dl3=hard-90
             if time.time() < dl3 - 5:
                hp,cp=continuous_polish(hb,n0,dl3,rng,seed+2,full=False)
                if cp < best_c: best_h,best_c=hp,cp
    # alts
    alts=[249,251,300,200,150,350]
    for nh in alts:
       avail=hard-100.0-time.time()
       if avail < 45.0: break
       if nh == n0: continue
       if n0 % 2 != 0 and nh % 2 != 0? ok
       slot=min(75.0, avail*0.45)
       if slot < 30.0: break
       dh=resample(best_h,best_h.size,nh)
       if nh == 300:
           dh=project_feasible(dh + 0.04*rng.randn(nh), nh*0.5)
       deadline_alt=time.time()+slot
       dh,ch=continuous_polish(dh,nh,deadline_alt,rng,seed+100+nh,full=(nh<=250))
       if ch < best_c:
           best_h,best_c=dh,ch
           # if even, quick binary? maybe if time left in slot? continuous used slot end; no.
    # final
    if time.time() < hard - 8:
        best_h,best_c=continuous_polish(best_h,best_h.size,hard-2,rng,seed+999,full=(best_h.size<=300))
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,best_h.size*0.5)
    best_c=c5c(corr_counts(best_h),best_h.size)
    return best_h,best_c,int(best_h.size)
```

Project water fill can be shorter:
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    if target<=0: return np.zeros(n)
    if target>=n: return np.ones(n)
    y=np.clip(x,0,1); target=float(target)
    for _ in range(30):
        s=y.sum(); d=target-s
        if abs(d)<1e-13: break
        if d>0:
            r=1-y; sr=r.sum()
            if sr<1e-15: break
            y=np.clip(y+d/sr*r,0,1)
        else:
            sy=y.sum()
            if sy<1e-15: break
            y=np.clip(y+d/sy*y,0,1)
    return y
```
No fallback; should be exact. Is it always Euclidean? If due to numerical not exact final, but sum error maybe 1e-13. The harness may require exact sum within tolerance. 30 iterations enough. If not exact due to zero room (infeasible) but target within [0,n], it will converge. Test: x all 0, target n/2. diff>0 room=n, y+=0.5, clip. diff 0. One iter. x all 0.9, target 0.5n? diff negative, sy=4.5n, y += (-0.4n)/(4.5n)*0.9 =0.8, exact. Good. If some hit bounds, few. We can trust. This saves 15 lines.

```
import numpy as np, time
from scipy.optimize import minimize
_TARGET=0.0
def _set_target(v):
    global _TARGET; _TARGET=float(v)
def _sum_fun(x): return x.sum()-_TARGET
def _sum_jac(x): return np.ones_like(x)
def corr_counts(h): return np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
def c5c(c,n): return float(c.max()*(2.0/n))
def project_feasible(x,target): ...
def make_binary(h,n): ...
def resample(h,n,nh): ...
def soft_objective(x,alpha,n): ...
def grad_weights(h,c,w,n): ...
def soft_gradient(x,alpha,n): ...
def iter_budget(n,deadline,cap): ...
def slsqp_soft(h,n,alpha,maxiter): ...
def active_descent(h,n,deadline,seed): ...
def build_add_matrix(h,n): ...
def binary_find_fast_move(cur,c,zeros,ones,center,R): ...
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,max_sweeps,restarts,seed): ...
def continuous_polish(h,n,deadline,seed,full): ...
```

```
def soft_objective(x,alpha,n):
    c=corr_counts(x)
    cm=float(c.max())
    z=alpha*(c-cm)
    s=float(np.exp(z).sum())
    return float(cm + np.log(s)/alpha)
```

```
for p in range(1,n):
```

Implement bisection robust:
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    target=float(target)
    if target <=0: return np.zeros(n)
    if target >= n: return np.ones(n)
    # Quick check: if clipped sum equal target, return clip
    y=np.clip(x,0,1)
    if abs(float(y.sum())-target) < 1e-12:
        return y
    span=max(1.0,float(np.max(np.abs(x)))) if n else 1.0
    lo=-span-1.0; hi=span+1.0
    for _ in range(60):
        mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
        val=float(np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0)))
        if val < target: lo=mid
        else: hi=mid
    return np.clip(x+0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
```
This is correct. 60 iterations maybe enough. Use 50? For precision 1e-15, span 10, 50 -> 8e-15. 50. Good.

If they require `h_values` length equals n_points, okay.

Now, if project bisection yields sum 124.49999999999999, check maybe pass. If strict exact? Can't guarantee floating. They likely tolerance.

Now, potential performance of `np.correlate` for float arrays: direct O(n^2). For n=350, 122k operations per c; active line search many. okay.

Let's final.
</think>

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_TARGET = 0.0

def _set_target(v):
    global _TARGET
    _TARGET = float(v)

def _sum_fun(x):
    return x.sum() - _TARGET

def _sum_jac(x):
    return np.ones_like(x, dtype=float)

def corr_counts(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")

def c5c(c, n):
    return float(c.max() * (2.0 / n))

def project_feasible(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return x
    target = float(target)
    if target <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n)
    if target >= float(n):
        return np.ones(n)
    y = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    if abs(float(y.sum()) - target) < 1e-13:
        return y
    span = max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(x))))
    lo = -span - 1.0
    hi = span + 1.0
    for _ in range(50):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        val = float(np.sum(np.clip(x + mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
        if val < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return np.clip(x + 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

def make_binary(h, n):
    hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    idx = np.argsort(h)[::-1][: n // 2]
    hb[idx] = 1.0
    return hb

def resample(h, n, nh):
    if nh == n:
        return project_feasible(h, n * 0.5)
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, nh)
    return project_feasible(np.interp(xn, xo, h), nh * 0.5)

def soft_objective(x, alpha, n):
    c = corr_counts(x)
    cm = float(c.max())
    z = float(alpha) * (c - cm)
    s = float(np.exp(z).sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return cm
    return float(cm + np.log(s) / float(alpha))

def grad_weights(h, c, w, n):
    G = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    center = n - 1
    w0 = w[center]
    if w0 != 0.0:
        G += w0 * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
    for p in range(1, n):
        wp = w[center + p]
        wn = w[center - p]
        if wp == 0.0 and wn == 0.0:
            continue
        a = n - p
        hr = h[p:]
        hl = h[:a]
        G[:a] += wn * (1.0 - hr) - wp * hr
        G[p:] += wp * (1.0 - hl) - wn * hl
    return G

def soft_gradient(x, alpha, n):
    c = corr_counts(x)
    cm = float(c.max())
    z = float(alpha) * (c - cm)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=float)
        w[n - 1] = 1.0
    else:
        w = ez / s
    return grad_weights(x, c, w, n)

def iter_budget(n, deadline, cap):
    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem < 10.0:
        return 0
    base = 0.04 if n <= 220 else (0.08 if n <= 320 else 0.15)
    it = int(rem / (base * 6.0))
    if it <= 0:
        return 0
    return min(int(cap), it)

def slsqp_soft(h, n, alpha, maxiter):
    target = n * 0.5
    _set_target(target)
    h0 = project_feasible(h, target)
    if maxiter <= 0:
        return h0
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [{"type": "eq", "fun": _sum_fun, "jac": _sum_jac}]
    try:
        res = minimize(
            soft_objective,
            h0,
            args=(float(alpha), int(n)),
            method="SLSQP",
            jac=soft_gradient,
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=cons,
            options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1e-13, "disp": False},
        )
        hx = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        hx = h0
    return project_feasible(hx, target)

def active_descent(h, n, deadline, seed):
    target = n * 0.5
    best = project_feasible(h, target)
    bestc = c5c(corr_counts(best), n)
    settings = (
        (0.25, 32, 0.08),
        (1.0, 64, 0.05),
        (4.0, 160, 0.03),
        (16.0, 400, 0.015),
    )
    for tol, ka, eta0 in settings:
        cur = best.copy()
        stall = 0
        while time.time() < deadline and stall < 60:
            c = corr_counts(cur)
            M = float(c.max())
            order = np.argsort(c)[::-1]
            near = np.where(c >= M - max(float(tol), 1e-12))[0]
            if near.size > ka:
                sel = order[:ka]
            else:
                sel = near
            if sel.size == 0:
                sel = order[:1]
            w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=float)
            w[sel] = 1.0 / float(sel.size)
            G = grad_weights(cur, c, w, n)
            gn = float(np.linalg.norm(G))
            if gn < 1e-14:
                stall += 5
                continue
            d = -G / gn
            improved = False
            for f in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625, 0.03125, 0.015625, 0.0078125):
                nh = project_feasible(cur + eta0 * f * d, target)
                cv = c5c(corr_counts(nh), n)
                if cv < bestc - 1e-14:
                    cur = nh
                    bestc = cv
                    best = nh.copy()
                    stall = 0
                    improved = True
                    break
            if not improved:
                stall += 1
    return best, bestc

def build_add_matrix(h, n):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    center = n - 1
    A = np.zeros((n, L), dtype=float)
    for p in range(1, n):
        a = n - p
        A[p:, center + p] += 1.0 - h[:a]
        A[:a, center + p] -= h[p:]
        A[:a, center - p] += 1.0 - h[p:]
        A[p:, center - p] -= h[:a]
    return A

def binary_find_fast_move(cur, c, zeros, ones, center, R):
    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return None
    n = cur.size
    L = len(c)
    R = int(min(R, L))
    if R <= 0:
        return None
    A = build_add_matrix(cur, n)
    Aones = A[ones]
    minVal = Aones.min(axis=0)
    minPos = np.argmin(Aones, axis=0)
    base = c[None, :] + A[zeros]
    score = base - minVal[None, :]
    if R < L:
        top = np.argpartition(score, L - R, axis=1)[:, L - R:]
    else:
        top = np.argsort(score, axis=1)[:, ::-1][:, :R]
    Jpos = minPos[top]
    J = ones[Jpos]
    AJ = A[J]
    delta = base[:, None, :] - AJ
    maxM = delta.max(axis=2)
    t1 = (center + zeros[:, None] - J).ravel()
    t2 = (center + J - zeros[:, None]).ravel()
    flat = delta.reshape(-1, L)
    idxp = np.arange(flat.shape[0])
    v1 = flat[idxp, t1]
    v2 = flat[idxp, t2]
    newmax = np.maximum(maxM.ravel(), np.maximum(v1 + 1.0, v2 + 1.0))
    m = int(np.argmin(newmax))
    r = m // R
    col = m % R
    return int(zeros[r]), int(J[r, col]), float(newmax[m])

def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline, max_sweeps, restarts, seed):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    hb = make_binary(h, n)
    c = corr_counts(hb)
    cur = float(c.max())
    bestc = cur * (2.0 / n)
    best = hb.copy()
    if n <= 250:
        R = 96
    elif n <= 320:
        R = 56
    else:
        R = 32
    for restart in range(restarts):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        if restart > 0:
            curh = best.copy()
            for _ in range(int(rng.randint(1, 4))):
                ones = np.where(curh > 0.5)[0]
                zeros = np.where(curh < 0.5)[0]
                if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
                    break
                a = int(rng.choice(ones))
                b = int(rng.choice(zeros))
                curh[a] = 0.0
                curh[b] = 1.0
            c = corr_counts(curh)
            cv = float(c.max()) * (2.0 / n)
            if cv > bestc + 0.05:
                curh = best.copy()
                c = corr_counts(curh)
            else:
                curh = curh.copy()
        else:
            curh = hb.copy()
            c = corr_counts(curh)
        cur = float(c.max())
        sweeps = 0
        while time.time() < deadline and sweeps < max_sweeps:
            sweeps += 1
            zeros = np.where(curh < 0.5)[0]
            ones = np.where(curh > 0.5)[0]
            mv = binary_find_fast_move(curh, c, zeros, ones, n - 1, R)
            if mv is None:
                break
            i, j, nv = mv
            if nv >= cur - 1e-12:
                break
            curh[i] = 1.0
            curh[j] = 0.0
            c = corr_counts(curh)
            cur = float(c.max())
            cv = cur * (2.0 / n)
            if cv < bestc:
                bestc = cv
                best = curh.copy()
    return best, bestc

def continuous_polish(h, n, deadline, seed, full=True):
    target = n * 0.5
    best = project_feasible(h, target)
    bestc = c5c(corr_counts(best), n)
    if full:
        alphas = (20.0, 100.0, 500.0, 2000.0, 8000.0)
        cap = 250 if n <= 300 else 90
    else:
        alphas = (20.0, 200.0, 2000.0)
        cap = 120 if n <= 300 else 60
    for a in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        it = iter_budget(n, deadline, cap)
        if it <= 0:
            break
        h2 = slsqp_soft(best, n, a, it)
        cv = c5c(corr_counts(h2), n)
        if cv < bestc - 1e-14:
            best = h2
            bestc = cv
    if time.time() < deadline - 5.0:
        best, bestc = active_descent(best, n, deadline, seed)
    return best, bestc

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    hard = t0 + budget - 5.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = int(h0.size)
    except Exception:
        n0 = 250
        h0 = np.zeros(n0, dtype=float)
        idx = rng.permutation(n0)
        h0[idx[: n0 // 2]] = 1.0

    if n0 <= 0 or n0 % 2 != 0 or n0 < 50 or n0 > 600:
        h0 = resample(h0, max(1, n0), 250)
        n0 = 250

    best_h = project_feasible(h0, n0 * 0.5)
    best_c = c5c(corr_counts(best_h), n0)

    if hard <= t0 + 3.0:
        return best_h, best_c, n0

    dl1 = min(t0 + 0.34 * budget, hard - 60.0)
    if dl1 > t0 + 2.0:
        best_h, best_c = continuous_polish(best_h, n0, dl1, seed, full=True)

    if n0 % 2 == 0:
        dl2 = min(t0 + 0.50 * budget, hard - 120.0)
        if time.time() < dl2 - 5.0:
            hb, hc = binary_local_search(best_h, n0, dl2, 3000, 4, seed + 1)
            if hc < best_c:
                best_h, best_c = hb, hc
            elif hc <= best_c + 0.015:
                dl3 = min(t0 + 0.58 * budget, hard - 90.0)
                if time.time() < dl3 - 5.0:
                    hp, cp = continuous_polish(hb, n0, dl3, seed + 2, full=False)
                    if cp < best_c:
                        best_h, best_c = hp, cp

    alts = (249, 251, 300, 200, 150, 350)
    for nh in alts:
        avail = hard - 100.0 - time.time()
        if avail < 45.0:
            break
        if nh == best_h.size:
            continue
        slot = min(75.0, avail * 0.45)
        if slot < 30.0:
            break
        dh = resample(best_h, best_h.size, nh)
        if nh == 300:
            dh = project_feasible(dh + 0.04 * rng.randn(nh), nh * 0.5)
        deadline_alt = time.time() + slot
        dh, ch = continuous_polish(dh, nh, deadline_alt, seed + 1000 + nh, full=(nh <= 250))
        if ch < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = dh, ch

    for eps in (1e-7, 1e-5, 1e-4, 1e-3):
        cand = best_h.copy()
        cand[cand < eps] = 0.0
        cand[cand > 1.0 - eps] = 1.0
        cand = project_feasible(cand, best_h.size * 0.5)
        cv = c5c(corr_counts(cand), best_h.size)
        if cv < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_h, best_c = cand, cv

    if time.time() < hard - 8.0:
        best_h, best_c = continuous_polish(
            best_h,
            best_h.size,
            hard - 2.0,
            seed + 9999,
            full=(best_h.size <= 300),
        )

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_h.size * 0.5)
    best_c = c5c(corr_counts(best_h), best_h.size)
    return best_h, best_c, int(best_h.size)
```
